I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` days of company profits, represented by values `a_i`
2. We need to divide these days into consecutive folders (groups of consecutive days)
3. Each folder can contain at most 2 days with negative profits (losses)
4. We want to minimize the number of folders
5. We need to output the minimum number of folders and the size of each folder

## Key Insights

1. Since we want to minimize the number of folders, we should try to make each folder as large as possible
2. A folder can have at most 2 negative values (losses)
3. We need to group consecutive days, so we can't jump around - days must be in consecutive order
4. This is essentially a greedy problem - we traverse the days from left to right and keep adding days to the current folder until adding the next day would create 3 or more negative values in that folder
5. When we reach a point where adding the next day would exceed 2 negative values, we must start a new folder

## Algorithm

1. Read the input: number of days `n` and the profit sequence
2. Initialize variables:
   - `folders` = list to store the size of each folder
   - `current_folder_size` = 0
   - `current_negative_count` = 0 (count of negative values in current folder)
3. Iterate through each day:
   - If adding this day would make the negative count exceed 2, start a new folder
   - Otherwise, add the day to the current folder
4. When we start a new folder, append the size of the previous folder to the result list
5. After processing all days, add the size of the last folder

Wait, let me reconsider the greedy approach. Since we want to minimize the number of folders, we should be greedy: whenever adding the next day would create 3 or more negative values in a folder, we must stop and start a new folder.

Actually, let me trace through the first example:
- Days: [1, 2, 3, -4, -5, -6, 5, -5, -6, -7, 6]
- Negative values at positions: 3, 4, 5, 7, 8, 9 (0-indexed)

If we try to pack days greedily:
- Folder 1: start at day 0, add days until we hit 3 negatives or reach the end
- Days 0-4: [1,2,3,-4,-5] → 2 negatives (-4,-5) → ok
- Next day (index 5): -6 → would make 3 negatives (-4,-5,-6) → not allowed
- So folder 1 ends at index 4 (days 0-4), size = 5
- Folder 2: start at day 5: [-6,5,-5] → negatives at -6 and -5 → 2 negatives → ok, size = 3
- Folder 3: start at day 8: [-6,-7,6] → negatives at -6,-7 → 2 negatives → ok, size = 3

This matches the expected output: 3 folders of sizes [5,3,3]

## Implementation Steps

1. Read input
2. Use a greedy approach: iterate through days, maintain current folder's negative count
3. When adding a day would make negative count ≥ 3, close current folder and start new one
4. Track the size of each folder
5. Output the number of folders and their sizes

```python
n = int(input())
profits = list(map(int, input().split()))

folders = []
current_negative_count = 0
folder_start = 0

for i in range(n):
    # Check if adding current day would exceed 2 negative values
    if profits[i] < 0:
        current_negative_count += 1
    
    # If we would have 3 or more negative values in current folder, need to start new folder
    if current_negative_count >= 3:
        # Close current folder (from folder_start to i-1)
        folder_size = i - folder_start
        folders.append(folder_size)
        
        # Start new folder from position i
        folder_start = i
        current_negative_count = 1  # Only count the current negative value in the new folder

# Don't forget the last folder
last_folder_size = n - folder_start
folders.append(last_folder_size)

print(len(folders))
print(*folders)
```

Wait, this has a bug. When we start a new folder, we're not properly accounting for the current day's negative value. Let me fix the logic.

Actually, when we detect that adding the current day would make negative count ≥ 3, we should close the current folder at position i-1, and then start a new folder at position i.

But in the loop, when we process day i, if it's negative, we add it to current_negative_count. So if adding it would make count ≥ 3, we need to close the folder before processing this day.

Let me revise:

```python

profits = list(map(int, input().split()))

        current_negative_count += 1

    if current_negative_count >= 3:
        # Close the current folder (from folder_start to i-1)
        folder_size = i - folder_start
        folders.append(folder_size)
        
        # Start new folder from position i
        folder_start = i
        current_negative_count = 1 if profits[i] < 0 else 0

# Add the last folder
last_folder_size = n - folder_start
folders.append(last_folder_size)

print(len(folders))
print(*folders)
```

Let me trace through the first example:
- profits = [1,2,3,-4,-5,-6,5,-5,-6,-7,6]
- i=0: profit=1 (not negative), current_negative_count=0 → continue
- i=1: profit=2 (not negative), current_negative_count=0 → continue  
- i=2: profit=3 (not negative), current_negative_count=0 → continue
- i=3: profit=-4 (negative), current_negative_count=1 → continue
- i=4: profit=-5 (negative), current_negative_count=2 → continue
- i=5: profit=-6 (negative), current_negative_count=3 → trigger closure
  - Close folder from start=0 to i-1=4 → size=5
  - Set folder_start=5, current_negative_count=1 (from -6)
- i=6: profit=5 (not negative), current_negative_count=1 → continue
- i=7: profit=-5 (negative), current_negative_count=2 → continue
- i=8: profit=-6 (negative), current_negative_count=3 → trigger closure
  - Close folder from start=5 to i-1=7 → size=3 (days 5,6,7)
  - Set folder_start=8, current_negative_count=1 (from -6)
- i=9: profit=-7 (negative), current_negative_count=2 → continue
- i=10: profit=6 (not negative), current_negative_count=2 → continue

- i=1: profit=-1, count=1 → continue
- i=2: profit=100, count=1 → continue
- i=3: profit=-1, count=2 → continue
- i=4: profit=0, count=2 → continue

```python

for i in range(n):
    # Check if current day is negative
    if profits[i] < 0:
        current_negative_count += 1

    if current_negative_count >= 3:
        # Close the current folder (from folder_start to i-1)
        folder_size = i - folder_start
        folders.append(folder_size)
        
        # Start a new folder from position i
        folder_start = i
        # Reset negative count for new folder (only count the current day if it's negative)
        if profits[i] < 0:
            current_negative_count = 1
        else:
            current_negative_count = 0

# Add the last folder
last_folder_size = n - folder_start
folders.append(last_folder_size)

print(len(folders))
print(*folders)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` days of data with profits/losses represented by integers
2. We need to divide the days into consecutive folders (segments)
3. Each folder can contain at most 2 days with negative values (losses)
4. We want to minimize the number of folders
5. We need to output the minimum number of folders and the sizes of each folder

## Key Insights

1. This is a greedy problem - we want to make each folder as long as possible while respecting the constraint of at most 2 negative days per folder
2. We can use a greedy approach: traverse the array from left to right and keep adding days to the current folder until we would exceed 2 negative days
3. When we reach 3 or more negative days in a folder, we must start a new folder
4. Since we want to minimize folders, we should always extend the current folder when possible

## Algorithm Steps

1. Read input: n and the array of daily profits
2. Initialize:
   - `folder_count = 1` (we start with at least one folder)
   - `current_folder_size = 0`
   - `current_negative_count = 0`
   - `result = []` (to store folder sizes)
3. Iterate through each day:
   - If the day has negative profit, increment negative count
   - If negative count reaches 3, we need to create a new folder
   - Otherwise, add the day to current folder
4. When we need to create a new folder, record the current folder size and reset current values
5. Add the last folder size if not already added

Wait, I need to be more careful. The constraint is that no folder should have 3 or more negative days. So we can continue adding days to a folder as long as we don't hit 3 negative days.

Actually, the greedy approach is:
- Start a folder
- Keep adding days until the current folder would contain 3 negative days
- When we would exceed 2 negative days, close the current folder and start a new one

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
profits = list(map(int, input().split()))

folders = []
current_negative_count = 0
current_folder_size = 0

for i in range(n):
    if profits[i] < 0:
        current_negative_count += 1
    
    # If adding this day would make negative count >= 3, we need a new folder
    if current_negative_count >= 3:
        # Close current folder and start new one
        folders.append(current_folder_size)
        current_folder_size = 1
        current_negative_count = 1  # reset negative count for new folder (this day has negative)
    else:
        current_folder_size += 1

# Don't forget to add the last folder
folders.append(current_folder_size)

# We go through each day and decide whether to add it to current folder or start a new one.

        current_negative_count = 1  # This day is negative, so count is 1

# i=10: a=6, neg=2, size=3

# Code:

n = int(input())
profits = list(map(int, input().split()))

folders = []
current_negative_count = 0
current_folder_size = 0

for i in range(n):
    if profits[i] < 0:
        current_negative_count += 1
    
    # If we have 3 or more negative days in current folder, we need to end this folder
    if current_negative_count >= 3:
        # Save current folder size and start a new one
        folders.append(current_folder_size)
        current_folder_size = 1
        current_negative_count = 1  # current day is negative
    else:
        current_folder_size += 1

# Don't forget the last folder
folders.append(current_folder_size)

print(len(folders))
print(*folders)
```

Wait, I think there's a logical error. When we add a day with negative profit, we increment the negative count, then check if it's >= 3. But when we start a new folder at a negative day, we set the negative count to 1, not to the count after adding.

Actually, in my trace, when we encounter a negative day that makes negative_count >= 3, we immediately close the current folder and start a new one. The negative count for the new folder will be 1 (since this day is negative). This is correct.

But in the code above, I'm adding to the folder size only when we don't close. When we close, we don't increment the size of the new folder yet - we start it with size 1, and then future days will be added.

The logic is: when we detect that adding the current day would make negative_count >= 3, we close the current folder (add its size to result), and start a new folder with just the current day (size=1, neg=1).

This is correct.

But let me verify the final example: Input 5, [0, -1, 100, -1, 0]
- Day 0: profit=0, not negative, so neg=0, size=1
- Day 1: profit=-1, neg=1, size=2
- Day 2: profit=100, neg=1, size=3
- Day 3: profit=-1, neg=2, size=4  
- Day 4: profit=0, neg=2, size=5
- At no point does neg >= 3, so we don't close any folder
- Final folders = [5]
- Output: "1\n5" - which matches expected!

Perfect.

```python
n = int(input())
profits = list(map(int, input().split()))

folders = []
current_negative_count = 0
current_folder_size = 0

for i in range(n):
    if profits[i] < 0:
        current_negative_count += 1

    if current_negative_count >= 3:

        current_folder_size = 1
        current_negative_count = 1  # Reset for the new folder, current day is negative
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Polycarpus has been working in the analytic department of the "F.R.A.U.D." company for as much as n days. Right now his task is to make a series of reports about the company's performance for the last n days. We know that the main information in a day report is value a_{i}, the company's profit on the i-th day. If a_{i} is negative, then the company suffered losses on the i-th day.

Polycarpus should sort the daily reports into folders. Each folder should include data on the company's performance for several consecutive days. Of course, the information on each of the n days should be exactly in one folder. Thus, Polycarpus puts information on the first few days in the first folder. The information on the several following days goes to the second folder, and so on.

It is known that the boss reads one daily report folder per day. If one folder has three or more reports for the days in which the company suffered losses (a_{i} < 0), he loses his temper and his wrath is terrible.

Therefore, Polycarpus wants to prepare the folders so that none of them contains information on three or more days with the loss, and the number of folders is minimal.

Write a program that, given sequence a_{i}, will print the minimum number of folders.


-----Input-----

The first line contains integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 100), n is the number of days. The second line contains a sequence of integers a_1, a_2, ..., a_{n} (|a_{i}| ≤ 100), where a_{i} means the company profit on the i-th day. It is possible that the company has no days with the negative a_{i}.


-----Output-----

Print an integer k — the required minimum number of folders. In the second line print a sequence of integers b_1, b_2, ..., b_{k}, where b_{j} is the number of day reports in the j-th folder.

If there are multiple ways to sort the reports into k days, print any of them.


-----Examples-----
Input
11
1 2 3 -4 -5 -6 5 -5 -6 -7 6

Output
3
5 3 3 
Input
5
0 -1 100 -1 0

Output
1
5 


-----Note-----

Here goes a way to sort the reports from the first sample into three folders:  1 2 3 -4 -5 | -6 5 -5 | -6 -7 6 

In the second sample you can put all five reports in one folder.

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have `n` days of company performance data (profits/losses)
2. We need to group consecutive days into folders
3. Each folder can contain at most 2 days with negative profit (losses)
4. We want to minimize the number of folders
5. We need to output the minimum number of folders and the sizes of each folder

## Key Insights

1. This is a greedy problem - we want to minimize folders, so we should try to pack as many consecutive days as possible into each folder without exceeding 2 negative days
2. For each folder, we can have at most 2 days with negative values
3. We process the days sequentially and form folders by taking consecutive days until we would exceed 2 negative days

## Algorithm

1. Read the input: n and the sequence of profits
2. Iterate through the days and keep track of the current folder's negative day count
3. For each day, if adding it to the current folder would make negative day count exceed 2, start a new folder
4. Record the size of each folder
5. Return the total number of folders and their sizes

## Step-by-step solution

Let me trace through the first example:
- Days: [1, 2, 3, -4, -5, -6, 5, -5, -6, -7, 6]
- First folder: 1,2,3,-4,-5 → negative days: -4,-5 (count=2) → valid, size=5
- Second folder: -6,5,-5 → negative days: -6,-5 (count=2) → valid, size=3  
- Third folder: -6,-7,6 → negative days: -6,-7 (count=2) → valid, size=3
- Total folders: 3

For the second example:
- Days: [0, -1, 100, -1, 0]
- Only 2 negative days (-1, -1), so one folder can hold both → total folders: 1

# We are at day i, and we want to add a[i]
# If adding a[i] would make negative_in_current > 2, then we cannot add it to the current folder

# Let me recheck the condition: we first increment negative_in_current if a[i] < 0, then check if negative_in_current > 2.

# This is correct - we check after adding the day.

# So the final algorithm is:

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

folders = []
current_start = 0
negative_in_current = 0

for i in range(n):
    # Add current day to current folder
    if a[i] < 0:
        negative_in_current += 1
    
    # If this would exceed 2 negative days, we must close the current folder
    if negative_in_current > 2:
        # Close the folder from current_start to i-1 (but actually we close before adding day i)
        # The folder includes indices from current_start to i-1
        folder_size = i - current_start  # because from s to i-1: (i-1 - s + 1) = i - s
        folders.append(folder_size)
        # Start a new folder from day i
        current_start = i
        negative_in_current = 1 if a[i] < 0 else 0

# After processing all days, add the last folder
if current_start < n:
    folders.append(n - current_start)

print(len(folders))
print(*folders)
```

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

folders = []
current_start = 0
negative_in_current = 0

for i in range(n):
    # Add the current day to the current folder
    if a[i] < 0:
        negative_in_current += 1
    
    # If adding this day would make more than 2 negative days in the current folder,
    # we must close the current folder before this day
    if negative_in_current > 2:
        # The current folder spans from current_start to i-1 (inclusive)
        folder_size = i - current_start
        folders.append(folder_size)
        # Start a new folder from day i
        current_start = i
        negative_in_current = 1 if a[i] < 0 else 0

# Add the last folder (from current_start to the end)
folders.append(n - current_start)

print(len(folders))
print(*folders)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Vasya’s elder brother Petya loves playing computer games. In one of his favourite computer games Petya reached the final level where a fight with the boss take place.

While playing the game Petya found spell scrolls and now he is about to use them. Let’s describe the way fighting goes on this level:

1) The boss has two parameters: max — the initial amount of health and reg — regeneration rate per second.

2) Every scroll also has two parameters: powi — spell power measured in percents — the maximal amount of health counted off the initial one, which allows to use the scroll (i.e. if the boss has more than powi percent of health the scroll cannot be used); and dmgi the damage per second inflicted upon the boss if the scroll is used. As soon as a scroll is used it disappears and another spell is cast upon the boss that inflicts dmgi of damage per second upon him until the end of the game.

During the battle the actions per second are performed in the following order: first the boss gets the damage from all the spells cast upon him, then he regenerates reg of health (at the same time he can’t have more than max of health), then the player may use another scroll (no more than one per second).

The boss is considered to be defeated if at the end of a second he has nonpositive ( ≤ 0) amount of health.

Help Petya to determine whether he can win with the set of scrolls available to him and if he can, determine the minimal number of seconds he needs to do it.

Input

The first line contains three integers N, max and reg (1 ≤ N, max, reg ≤ 1000) –– the amount of scrolls and the parameters of the boss. The next N lines contain two integers powi and dmgi each — the parameters of the i-th scroll (0 ≤ powi ≤ 100, 1 ≤ dmgi ≤ 2000). 

Output

In case Petya can’t complete this level, output in the single line NO.

Otherwise, output on the first line YES. On the second line output the minimal time after which the boss can be defeated and the number of used scrolls. In the next lines for each used scroll output space-separated number of seconds passed from the start of the battle to the moment the scroll was used and the number of the scroll. Scrolls are numbered starting from 1 in the input order. The first scroll is considered to be available to be used after 0 seconds.

Output scrolls in the order they were used. It is not allowed to use scrolls after the boss is defeated.

Examples

Input

2 10 3
100 3
99 1


Output

NO


Input

2 100 10
100 11
90 9


Output

YES
19 2
0 1
10 2

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout


Now solve the problem and return the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

## Problem Understanding

1. **Boss parameters**: 
   - `max`: initial health
   - `reg`: regeneration rate per second (health restored each second)

2. **Scroll parameters**:
   - `powi`: maximum health percentage allowed to use the scroll (if current health > powi%, scroll can't be used)
   - `dmgi`: damage per second inflicted when scroll is used

3. **Battle mechanics**:
   - Each second: 
     1. All active spells damage the boss
     2. Boss regenerates `reg` health (capped at `max`)
     3. Player can use one scroll (if conditions allow)
   - Boss is defeated when health ≤ 0 at end of any second

4. **Goal**: Find minimal time to defeat boss, or output "NO" if impossible.

## Key Insights

1. We need to simulate the battle second by second
2. For each second, we check if we can use any available scroll
3. A scroll can be used only if the boss's current health ≤ powi% of max
4. When a scroll is used, it adds dmgi damage per second to the boss
5. We want to minimize total time, so we should use scrolls optimally

## Strategy

1. **Sort scrolls by powi in ascending order** - scrolls with lower powi requirements are more flexible (can be used when boss has more health)
2. **Greedy approach**: At each second, use the scroll that:
   - Has the smallest powi requirement (most flexible)
   - And provides the highest dmgi (most damage)
3. **Simulation**: For each second:
   - Apply all active damage
   - Apply regeneration (capped at max)
   - Check if boss health ≤ 0 (defeated)
   - If not defeated, check which scrolls can be used and apply the best one

Now, for the implementation:

1. Read N, max, reg
2. Read N scrolls: store as (pow, dmg, index)
3. Sort scrolls by dmg in descending order (for selection when available)
4. Initialize:
   - health = max
   - total_damage = 0
   - used = [False] * N
   - time = 0
5. While True:
   - If health <= 0: break and output time
   - For each second, we are at time 'time'
   - At the beginning of this second, health is current health
   - Apply damage: new_health = health - total_damage
   - If new_health <= 0: break and output time
   - Otherwise: health = min(new_health + reg, max)
   - Now, among unused scrolls, find those with current_health <= pow * max / 100
   - Among those, choose the one with highest dmgi
   - If found, use it: total_damage += dmgi, used[i] = True, time += 1
   - Else, time += 1 (no scroll used, continue)
6. If we exit the loop without health<=0, then output "NO"

We need to be careful with the time: at the beginning of second 0, time=0.

After each second, time increases by 1.

In the loop, for each second, we do the actions.

At the beginning of second t, we have a health value.

We then do the actions for that second.

Then, if we used a scroll, we move to next second.

Otherwise, we also move to next second.

So we increase time by 1 after each second.

In the code, we can do:

time = 0
while True:
    # at beginning of second 'time'
    if health <= 0:
        break
    # apply damage
    net_damage = health - total_damage
    if net_damage <= 0:
        break
    health = net_damage + reg
    health = min(health, max)
    
    # now check if we can use a scroll at this second
    best_dmg = -1
    best_idx = -1
    for i in range(N):
        if not used[i] and health <= pow[i] * max / 100:
            if dmg[i] > best_dmg:
                best_dmg = dmg[i]
                best_idx = i
    if best_idx != -1:
        total_damage += dmg[best_idx]
        used[best_idx] = True
        time += 1  # we use a scroll at this second, so move to next second
    else:
        time += 1  # no scroll used, still move to next second

After the loop, if health > 0 and time is large, then output "NO"

But we break when health <= 0, so after the loop, if health > 0 and we've used all scrolls, output "NO"

We need to check if we have used all scrolls or not.

Actually, in the loop, if we can't use any scroll and we have not used all, we continue.

But we might need to cap the time to avoid infinite loop.

Since the health is bounded and net damage per second is at least 0 (if total_damage - reg >=0 then health will eventually drop), and if total_damage - reg < 0, health will increase, so we will never defeat.

In that case, we will loop infinitely.

We can add a time limit, e.g., time > 10000, then output "NO"

Given the constraints (max, reg, dmgi) up to 1000, and N up to 1000, a safe limit is 100000.

Let me implement accordingly.

Note: when health <= powi * max / 100, it is a float comparison.

To avoid floating point errors, we can compare: health <= powi * max / 100.0

But in integer arithmetic, health <= (powi * max) / 100

Since health is integer, and powi * max might not be divisible by 100, we can use:

health * 100 <= powi * max

This avoids floating point.

For a scroll with powi, we can use it at time if health * 100 <= powi * max

Yes, because health <= (powi * max) / 100 is equivalent to health * 100 <= powi * max when health is integer.

Therefore, we can use integer comparison.

Final implementation:

```python
def solve():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read
    data = input().split()
    
    # Parse first line
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index+1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i+1))  # (pow, dmg, original_index)
    
    # If no scrolls, check if we can defeat immediately
    if n == 0:
        if max_health <= 0:
            print("YES")
            print(0, 0)
        else:
            print("NO")
        return
    
    # We'll simulate second by second
    health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used = [False] * n
    time = 0
    max_time = 100000  # safety cap
    
    # We'll store the use times and which scroll is used when
    use_times = []
    scroll_usage = []  # (time, scroll_index)
    
    while time < max_time:
        # Check if already defeated at beginning of second
        if health <= 0:
            break
            
        # At the beginning of second `time`, health is current health
        # Apply damage from all active spells
        damage_after = health - total_damage
        if damage_after <= 0:
            # Boss is defeated at the end of this second
            break
            
        # Apply regeneration
        health = damage_after + reg
        health = min(health, max_health)
        
        # Now, check if we can use any scroll at this second (time)
        best_dmg = -1
        best_idx = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if not used[i]:
                powi, dmgi, orig_idx = scrolls[i]
                # Check if health <= powi * max / 100
                if health * 100 <= powi * max_health:
                    if dmgi > best_dmg:
                        best_dmg = dmgi
                        best_idx = i
        
        if best_idx != -1:
            # Use the scroll
            total_damage += scrolls[best_idx][1]
            used[best_idx] = True
            use_times.append(time)
            scroll_usage.append((time, scrolls[best_idx][2]))  # (time, original_index)
            time += 1
        else:
            time += 1
    
    # Check if we defeated the boss
    if health <= 0:
        print("YES")
        print(time, len(scroll_usage))
        for t, s in scroll_usage:
            print(t, s)
    else:
        print("NO")

solve()
```

Wait, in the loop, when we break because health <= 0, we break before the damage and regeneration.

In the while loop, at the beginning of second `time`, we check if health <= 0.

If yes, we break and output time.

But in the code above, we have a condition at the beginning of the loop: if health <= 0: break.

But in the simulation, after we apply damage and regeneration, health might be >0, but if at the beginning of a second, health <=0, then we should have output that second.

Actually, health should be >0 at the beginning of each second because we only update it after damage and regeneration, and we only update it when we do the actions.

In the loop, at the beginning of second time, health is the health from the previous second.

We might have health <=0 at the beginning of a second, which means the boss was already defeated, so we should have broken earlier.

In the simulation, we should only enter a second if health >0.

Therefore, we should remove the if health <=0 at the beginning.

The only way health becomes <=0 is after damage.

So in the loop, at the beginning of second time, health >0.

We then apply damage: if health - total_damage <=0, then we break and output time.

Otherwise, we apply regeneration and then check for scroll.

So the code should be:

```python
def solve():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read
    data = input().split()
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])

        dmgi = int(data[index+1])

    if n == 0:
        if max_health <= 0:
            print("YES")
            print(0, 0)
        else:
            print("NO")
        return
    
    health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used = [False] * n
    time = 0

    scroll_usage = []

        damage_after = health - total_damage

            # Boss is defeated at the end of this second
            print("YES")
            print(time, len(use_times))
            for t, s in scroll_usage:
                print(t, s)

        health = min(health, max_health)

        best_dmg = -1

    print("NO")
```

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))  # (pow, dmg, original_index)
    
    if n == 0:
        if max_health <= 0:
            print("YES")
            print(0, 0)
        else:
            print("NO")
        return
    
    health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used = [False] * n
    time = 0
    max_time = 100000
    
    use_times = []
    scroll_usage = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    
    while time < max_time:
        # Apply damage from active spells
        damage_after = health - total_damage
        if damage_after <= 0:
            # Boss is defeated at the end of this second
            print("YES")
            print(time, len(use_times))
            for t, s in scroll_usage:
                print(t, s)
            return
        
        # Apply regeneration
        health = damage_after + reg
        health = min(health, max_health)
        
        # Check for available scrolls at this second
        best_dmg = -1
        best_idx = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if not used[i]:
                powi, dmgi, orig_idx = scrolls[i]
                # health <= powi * max_health / 100  -> health * 100 <= powi * max_health
                if health * 100 <= powi * max_health:
                    if dmgi > best_dmg:
                        best_dmg = dmgi
                        best_idx = i
        
        if best_idx != -1:
            total_damage += scrolls[best_idx][1]
            used[best_idx] = True
            use_times.append(time)
            scroll_usage.append((time, scrolls[best_idx][2]))
            time += 1
        else:
            time += 1
    
    # If we reach here, we haven't defeated the boss within max_time
    print("NO")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Vasya’s elder brother Petya loves playing computer games. In one of his favourite computer games Petya reached the final level where a fight with the boss take place.

While playing the game Petya found spell scrolls and now he is about to use them. Let’s describe the way fighting goes on this level:

1) The boss has two parameters: max — the initial amount of health and reg — regeneration rate per second.

2) Every scroll also has two parameters: powi — spell power measured in percents — the maximal amount of health counted off the initial one, which allows to use the scroll (i.e. if the boss has more than powi percent of health the scroll cannot be used); and dmgi the damage per second inflicted upon the boss if the scroll is used. As soon as a scroll is used it disappears and another spell is cast upon the boss that inflicts dmgi of damage per second upon him until the end of the game.

During the battle the actions per second are performed in the following order: first the boss gets the damage from all the spells cast upon him, then he regenerates reg of health (at the same time he can’t have more than max of health), then the player may use another scroll (no more than one per second).

The boss is considered to be defeated if at the end of a second he has nonpositive ( ≤ 0) amount of health.

Help Petya to determine whether he can win with the set of scrolls available to him and if he can, determine the minimal number of seconds he needs to do it.

Input

The first line contains three integers N, max and reg (1 ≤ N, max, reg ≤ 1000) –– the amount of scrolls and the parameters of the boss. The next N lines contain two integers powi and dmgi each — the parameters of the i-th scroll (0 ≤ powi ≤ 100, 1 ≤ dmgi ≤ 2000). 

Output

In case Petya can’t complete this level, output in the single line NO.

Otherwise, output on the first line YES. On the second line output the minimal time after which the boss can be defeated and the number of used scrolls. In the next lines for each used scroll output space-separated number of seconds passed from the start of the battle to the moment the scroll was used and the number of the scroll. Scrolls are numbered starting from 1 in the input order. The first scroll is considered to be available to be used after 0 seconds.

Output scrolls in the order they were used. It is not allowed to use scrolls after the boss is defeated.

Examples

Input

2 10 3
100 3
99 1


Output

NO


Input

2 100 10
100 11
90 9


Output

YES
19 2
0 1
10 2

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a boss with:
   - `max`: initial health
   - `reg`: health regeneration per second (can't exceed max)

2. We have N scrolls, each with:
   - `powi`: maximum percentage of initial health that can be present when using the scroll
   - `dmgi`: damage per second when the scroll is used

3. Game timeline per second:
   - First: all active spells deal damage to boss
   - Second: boss regenerates `reg` health (capped at `max`)
   - Third: player can use one scroll (if conditions are met)

4. Boss is defeated when health becomes ≤ 0 at the end of any second.

5. We need to find the minimal time to defeat the boss, or output "NO" if impossible.

## Key Insights

1. We need to strategically choose when to use scrolls to minimize total time.

2. For a scroll to be usable at time `t`, the boss's health at time `t` must be ≤ `powi`% of `max`.

3. The boss's health changes over time based on:
   - Damage from active spells
   - Regeneration (at start of each second)

4. We should use scrolls with higher damage when possible, and use them at the earliest possible time when conditions allow.

5. Since we can only use one scroll per second, we need to simulate the battle second by second.

## Strategy

1. Sort scrolls by damage in descending order (higher damage first) - we want to prioritize powerful scrolls.

2. For each possible time, simulate the battle and check if we can defeat the boss.

3. Use greedy approach: at each second, if we can use any available scroll (meeting the power condition), use the one with highest damage.

4. However, the order of scroll usage might matter - we should try to use stronger scrolls earlier when possible.

5. Since we want minimum time, we can try to simulate from time 0 upward until we defeat the boss.

## Algorithm

1. Calculate the boss's health over time with initial conditions.
2. For each second, check which scrolls can be used (health ≤ powi% of max).
3. Among usable scrolls, use the one with highest dmgi (if multiple).
4. Update the damage per second list.
5. Check if boss health becomes ≤ 0 at the end of any second.

Let me implement this:

```python
def solve():
    n, max_health, reg = map(int, input().split())
    scrolls = []
    for i in range(n):
        powi, dmgi = map(int, input().split())
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))  # (power_percentage, damage_per_second, scroll_number)
    
    # Sort scrolls by damage in descending order to prioritize stronger ones
    scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
    
    # Try different time values starting from 0
    # The maximum time we might need is bounded by the problem constraints
    # Since max_health <= 1000, reg >= 1, we can safely limit to a reasonable upper bound
    max_time = 10000  # Conservative upper bound
    
    # We'll try to find the minimum time by simulating second by second
    for time in range(max_time + 1):
        # Simulate the battle up to this time
        current_health = max_health
        active_damage = 0
        used_scrolls = []
        scroll_usage_times = []
        
        # Check if we can defeat the boss at time `time` by using scrolls optimally
        temp_health = max_health
        temp_damage = 0
        scroll_used_at = []
        
        # Simulate each second from 0 to time
        for second in range(time + 1):
            # First: damage from active spells
            temp_health -= temp_damage
            
            # If health becomes ≤ 0, we've defeated the boss at this second
            if temp_health <= 0:
                # Check if we can achieve this state with valid scroll usage
                # We need to verify that at each second when we used a scroll, the condition was met
                valid = True
                
                # Reconstruct the sequence of scroll usage
                scroll_time_list = []
                current_health = max_health
                current_damage = 0
                scroll_usage_times = []
                
                for s in range(second + 1):
                    # Apply damage from active spells
                    current_health -= current_damage
                    
                    # If health ≤ 0, we're done
                    if current_health <= 0:
                        break
                    
                    # Check if we can use a scroll at this second
                    # We need to decide which scroll to use, prioritizing by damage
                    usable_scrolls = []
                    for powi, dmg, scroll_num in scrolls:
                        # Check if current health is ≤ powi% of max_health
                        if current_health <= (powi * max_health) // 100:
                            usable_scrolls.append((powi, dmg, scroll_num))
                    
                    # If there are usable scrolls, use the one with highest damage
                    if usable_scrolls:
                        usable_scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
                        chosen_scroll = usable_scrolls[0]
                        current_damage += chosen_scroll[1]
                        scroll_usage_times.append((s, chosen_scroll[2]))  # (second, scroll_number)
                        
                        # Remove this scroll from available scrolls
                        # But we're checking all possible sequences, so we need to track usage
                        # Instead, let's just try to find a valid sequence that works
                        # We need to simulate properly
                    
                # Try greedy simulation with the best available scroll each time
                current_health = max_health
                current_damage = 0
                scroll_time_list = []
                
                for s in range(second + 1):
                    # Damage phase
                    current_health -= current_damage
                    
                    # If health ≤ 0, we win at this second
                    if current_health <= 0:
                        break
                        
                    # Regeneration phase
                    current_health = min(current_health + reg, max_health)
                    
                    # Check if we can use any scroll at this second (after regeneration)
                    usable_scrolls = []
                    for powi, dmg, scroll_num in scrolls:
                        if current_health <= (powi * max_health) // 100:
                            usable_scrolls.append((powi, dmg, scroll_num))
                    
                    # If usable scrolls exist, use the one with highest damage
                    if usable_scrolls:
                        usable_scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
                        chosen_scroll = usable_scrolls[0]
                        current_damage += chosen_scroll[1]
                        scroll_time_list.append((s, chosen_scroll[2]))
                
                # After simulation, check if boss health became ≤ 0 at some point
                # But we need to run the full simulation properly
                
                # Let me restart with a clearer simulation
                break
        else:
            continue
        break
    
    # Let me implement a cleaner simulation
    # For each possible time, simulate the battle optimally
    
    # Try to find minimum time using greedy strategy
    current_health = max_health
    active_damage = 0
    used_scrolls = []
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Vasya’s elder brother Petya loves playing computer games. In one of his favourite computer games Petya reached the final level where a fight with the boss take place.

While playing the game Petya found spell scrolls and now he is about to use them. Let’s describe the way fighting goes on this level:

1) The boss has two parameters: max — the initial amount of health and reg — regeneration rate per second.

2) Every scroll also has two parameters: powi — spell power measured in percents — the maximal amount of health counted off the initial one, which allows to use the scroll (i.e. if the boss has more than powi percent of health the scroll cannot be used); and dmgi the damage per second inflicted upon the boss if the scroll is used. As soon as a scroll is used it disappears and another spell is cast upon the boss that inflicts dmgi of damage per second upon him until the end of the game.

During the battle the actions per second are performed in the following order: first the boss gets the damage from all the spells cast upon him, then he regenerates reg of health (at the same time he can’t have more than max of health), then the player may use another scroll (no more than one per second).

The boss is considered to be defeated if at the end of a second he has nonpositive ( ≤ 0) amount of health.

Help Petya to determine whether he can win with the set of scrolls available to him and if he can, determine the minimal number of seconds he needs to do it.

Input

The first line contains three integers N, max and reg (1 ≤ N, max, reg ≤ 1000) –– the amount of scrolls and the parameters of the boss. The next N lines contain two integers powi and dmgi each — the parameters of the i-th scroll (0 ≤ powi ≤ 100, 1 ≤ dmgi ≤ 2000). 

Output

In case Petya can’t complete this level, output in the single line NO.

Otherwise, output on the first line YES. On the second line output the minimal time after which the boss can be defeated and the number of used scrolls. In the next lines for each used scroll output space-separated number of seconds passed from the start of the battle to the moment the scroll was used and the number of the scroll. Scrolls are numbered starting from 1 in the input order. The first scroll is considered to be available to be used after 0 seconds.

Output scrolls in the order they were used. It is not allowed to use scrolls after the boss is defeated.

Examples

Input

2 10 3
100 3
99 1


Output

NO


Input

2 100 10
100 11
90 9


Output

YES
19 2
0 1
10 2

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

## Problem Understanding

1. **Boss Parameters**: 
   - `max`: initial health
   - `reg`: regeneration rate per second (health restored each second)

2. **Scroll Parameters**:
   - `powi`: maximum percentage of initial health allowed to use the scroll
   - `dmgi`: damage per second inflicted when the scroll is used

3. **Battle Timing**:
   - Each second follows this order:
     1. All active spells deal damage to boss
     2. Boss regenerates `reg` health (capped at `max`)
     3. Player can use one scroll (if conditions met)

4. **Win Condition**: Boss has ≤ 0 health at end of some second

5. **Constraints**:
   - Can use at most one scroll per second
   - Cannot use scroll if boss health > `powi`% of `max`
   - Output the minimal time and which scrolls to use

3. After using a scroll, it continuously deals `dmgi` damage per second (until game end).

4. The damage is applied at the end of each second after regeneration.

```python
def solve():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read
    data = input().split()
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg_rate = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))  # (powi, dmgi, original_index)
    
    # Sort scrolls by damage in descending order to prefer higher damage
    scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
    
    # We'll simulate second by second
    current_health = max_health
    current_damage = 0  # total damage from active spells
    used_scrolls = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    time = 0
    
    # We'll simulate until we either defeat the boss or run out of scrolls
    max_simulations = 10000  # safety bound
    
    while time < max_simulations:
        # Apply damage from active spells
        current_health -= current_damage
        
        # If health becomes negative, we're already defeated
        if current_health <= 0:
            break
            
        # Apply regeneration
        current_health = min(current_health + reg_rate, max_health)
        
        # Check which scrolls can be used (health <= powi% of max_health)
        available_scrolls = []
        for powi, dmgi, orig_idx in scrolls:
            if current_health <= powi * max_health / 100:
                available_scrolls.append((dmgi, orig_idx, powi))
        
        # If no scroll available, we can't proceed further
        if not available_scrolls:
            # Check if we've already defeated the boss
            if current_health <= 0:
                break
            # Otherwise, we can't use any more scrolls, so we're stuck
            break
            
        # Choose the scroll with highest damage
        available_scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
        chosen_damage, chosen_idx, chosen_powi = available_scrolls[0]
        
        # Apply the chosen scroll
        current_damage += chosen_damage
        used_scrolls.append((time, chosen_idx))
        
        # Move to next second
        time += 1
        
        # Check if boss is defeated at end of this second
        # After damage, before regeneration
        if current_health <= 0:
            break
    
    if current_health <= 0:
        print("YES")
        print(time, len(used_scrolls))
        for time_used, scroll_idx in used_scrolls:
            print(time_used, scroll_idx)
    else:
        print("NO")

solve()
```

- After t=19: health after damage = 100 - 9*1 - 9*10 = 100 - 9 - 90 = 1? Let me recalculate

- Start at t=10: health after damage = 80 (before regeneration) → after regen 90
- t=11: after damage 90-20=70, after regen 80
- t=12: 80-20=60, after regen 70
- Each second: health after damage decreases by 20, then increases by 10 → net -10 per second

So after 9 more seconds (t=11 to t=19):
- health at end of t=19 after damage: 90 - 10*9 = 0
- So after damage: 0 → boss defeated!

This is not right. The health should be decreasing.

At t=0: health = 100
After first second: damage 11 → 100-11=89 → +10 → 99
After second second: 99-11=88 → +10 → 98
After k seconds: health after regeneration = 100 - k*1 = 100 - k

At t=9: health after regeneration = 100 - 9 = 91
At t=10: before damage: 91, after damage: 91 - 11 = 80, after regen: 80 + 10 = 90

Yes! At t=10, after damage and regeneration, health = 90.

Can we use scroll 2? 90 ≤ 90 → yes!

Now damage = 11 + 9 = 20

- Before damage: health = 90 (after t=10 regeneration)
- After damage: 90 - 20 = 70
- After regen: 70 + 10 = 80

t=12:
- Before damage: 80
- After damage: 80 - 20 = 60
- After regen: 60 + 10 = 70

Each second net change: -10

So after t seconds from t=10:
- health after damage at end of t: 90 - 10*(t-10)

t=19:
- After damage: 90 - 10*(19-10) = 90 - 90 = 0
- So after damage at t=19, health = 0 → boss is defeated!

Yes! So at the end of t=19, health becomes 0, so we win.

So the simulation should be:

1. For each second t from 0, 1, 2, ...:
   a. Apply all current damage to health (h -= total_damage)
   b. If h ≤ 0, return (t, used_scrolls)
   c. Else, h = min(h + reg_rate, max_health)
   d. Check which scrolls can be used (current h ≤ powi * max_health / 100)
   e. Among available, choose one with highest damage
   f. Add that scroll to used_scrolls
   g. Increase total_damage by that scroll's dmgi
   h. t += 1

In the simulation, at time t, we apply damage, check if defeated, then regenerate, then possibly use a scroll.

But the time in our loop is the time of each second.

Let me implement this:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        print("NO")
        return
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg_rate = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))
    
    # We'll simulate second by second
    current_health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used_scrolls = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    time = 0
    
    # Maximum simulation steps - since health can't go below 0, and damage increases, 
    # but in worst case health might be high, so set a reasonable bound
    MAX_SIMULATION = 10000
    
    while time < MAX_SIMULATION:
        # Apply damage from active spells
        current_health -= total_damage
        
        # Check if boss is defeated
        if current_health <= 0:
            print("YES")
            print(time, len(used_scrolls))
            for t, s in used_scrolls:
                print(t, s)
            return
        
        # Regenerate health
        current_health = min(current_health + reg_rate, max_health)
        
        # Check which scrolls can be used (health <= powi% of max_health)
        available_scrolls = []
        for powi, dmgi, orig_idx in scrolls:
            if current_health <= powi * max_health / 100:
                available_scrolls.append((dmgi, orig_idx))
        
        if not available_scrolls:
            # No scroll can be used, but we might have already defeated boss
            # If we didn't, we can't proceed further
            break
            
        # Choose the scroll with highest damage
        available_scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
        chosen_dmgi, chosen_idx = available_scrolls[0]
        
        # Use this scroll
        used_scrolls.append((time, chosen_idx))
        total_damage += chosen_dmgi
        
        time += 1
    
    # If we get here, we couldn't defeat the boss
    print("NO")

main()
```

The problem says: "the amount of scrolls and the parameters of the boss" - so we have exactly N scrolls available.

And "another spell is cast upon the boss" - implies each scroll can be used only once.

I missed that!

In example 1, we have only 2 scrolls.

In my simulation, I was using scroll 1 repeatedly, but we can only use each scroll once.

That's the key constraint.

I need to only use each scroll once.

So in the simulation, each scroll can be used at most once.

Let me modify the algorithm:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        print("NO")
        return
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg_rate = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))
    
    # We'll simulate second by second
    current_health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used_scrolls = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    time = 0
    
    # Track which scrolls have been used
    used_indices = set()
    
    # Maximum simulation steps
    MAX_SIMULATION = 10000
    
    while time < MAX_SIMULATION:
        # Apply damage from active spells
        current_health -= total_damage
        
        # Check if boss is defeated
        if current_health <= 0:
            print("YES")
            print(time, len(used_scrolls))
            for t, s in used_scrolls:
                print(t, s)
            return
        
        # Regenerate health
        current_health = min(current_health + reg_rate, max_health)
        
        # Check which scrolls can be used (current health <= powi% of max_health)
        # and which haven't been used yet
        available_scrolls = []
        for i in range(n):
            powi, dmgi, orig_idx = scrolls[i]
            if i not in used_indices and current_health <= powi * max_health / 100:
                available_scrolls.append((dmgi, orig_idx, i))
        
        if not available_scrolls:
            break
            
        # Choose the scroll with highest damage among available
        available_scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
        chosen_dmgi, chosen_idx, chosen_i = available_scrolls[0]
        
        # Use this scroll
        used_scrolls.append((time, chosen_idx))
        used_indices.add(chosen_i)
        total_damage += chosen_dmgi
        
        time += 1
    
    # If we get here, we couldn't defeat the boss
    print("NO")

main()
```

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        print("NO")
        return
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg_rate = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))
    
    # We'll simulate second by second
    current_health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used_scrolls = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    time = 0
    
    # Track which scrolls have been used
    used_indices = set()
    
    # Maximum simulation steps
    MAX_SIMULATION = 10000
    
    while time < MAX_SIMULATION:
        # Apply damage from active spells
        current_health -= total_damage
        
        # Check if boss is defeated
        if current_health <= 0:
            print("YES")
            print(time, len(used_scrolls))
            for t, s in used_scrolls:
                print(t, s)
            return
        
        # Regenerate health
        current_health = min(current_health + reg_rate, max_health)
        
        # Check which scrolls can be used (current health <= powi% of max_health)
        # and which haven't been used yet
        available_scrolls = []
        for i in range(n):
            powi, dmgi, orig_idx = scrolls[i]
            if i not in used_indices and current_health <= powi * max_health / 100:
                available_scrolls.append((dmgi, orig_idx, i))
        
        if not available_scrolls:
            break
            
        # Choose the scroll with highest damage among available
        available_scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
        chosen_dmgi, chosen_idx, chosen_i = available_scrolls[0]
        
        # Use this scroll
        used_scrolls.append((time, chosen_idx))
        used_indices.add(chosen_i)
        total_damage += chosen_dmgi
        
        time += 1
    
    # If we get here, we couldn't defeat the boss
    print("NO")

main()
```

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        print("NO")
        return
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg_rate = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))
    
    # We'll simulate second by second
    current_health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used_scrolls = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    time = 0
    
    # Track which scrolls have been used
    used_indices = set()
    
    # Maximum simulation steps
    MAX_SIMULATION = 10000
    
    while time < MAX_SIMULATION:
        # Apply damage from active spells
        current_health -= total_damage
        
        # Check if boss is defeated
        if current_health <= 0:
            print("YES")
            print(time, len(used_scrolls))
            for t, s in used_scrolls:
                print(t, s)
            return
        
        # Regenerate health
        current_health = min(current_health + reg_rate, max_health)
        
        # Check which scrolls can be used (current health <= powi% of max_health)
        # and which haven't been used yet

        for i in range(n):
            powi, dmgi, orig_idx = scrolls[i]
            if i not in used_indices and current_health <= powi * max_health / 100:
                available_scrolls.append((dmgi, orig_idx, i))

        used_indices.add(chosen_i)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Vasya’s elder brother Petya loves playing computer games. In one of his favourite computer games Petya reached the final level where a fight with the boss take place.

While playing the game Petya found spell scrolls and now he is about to use them. Let’s describe the way fighting goes on this level:

1) The boss has two parameters: max — the initial amount of health and reg — regeneration rate per second.

2) Every scroll also has two parameters: powi — spell power measured in percents — the maximal amount of health counted off the initial one, which allows to use the scroll (i.e. if the boss has more than powi percent of health the scroll cannot be used); and dmgi the damage per second inflicted upon the boss if the scroll is used. As soon as a scroll is used it disappears and another spell is cast upon the boss that inflicts dmgi of damage per second upon him until the end of the game.

During the battle the actions per second are performed in the following order: first the boss gets the damage from all the spells cast upon him, then he regenerates reg of health (at the same time he can’t have more than max of health), then the player may use another scroll (no more than one per second).

The boss is considered to be defeated if at the end of a second he has nonpositive ( ≤ 0) amount of health.

Help Petya to determine whether he can win with the set of scrolls available to him and if he can, determine the minimal number of seconds he needs to do it.

Input

The first line contains three integers N, max and reg (1 ≤ N, max, reg ≤ 1000) –– the amount of scrolls and the parameters of the boss. The next N lines contain two integers powi and dmgi each — the parameters of the i-th scroll (0 ≤ powi ≤ 100, 1 ≤ dmgi ≤ 2000). 

Output

In case Petya can’t complete this level, output in the single line NO.

Otherwise, output on the first line YES. On the second line output the minimal time after which the boss can be defeated and the number of used scrolls. In the next lines for each used scroll output space-separated number of seconds passed from the start of the battle to the moment the scroll was used and the number of the scroll. Scrolls are numbered starting from 1 in the input order. The first scroll is considered to be available to be used after 0 seconds.

Output scrolls in the order they were used. It is not allowed to use scrolls after the boss is defeated.

Examples

Input

2 10 3
100 3
99 1


Output

NO


Input

2 100 10
100 11
90 9


Output

YES
19 2
0 1
10 2

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a boss with initial health `max` and regeneration rate `reg` per second
2. We have N scroll spells, each with:
   - `powi`: maximum percentage of initial health that can be used (if current health > powi%, can't use)
   - `dmgi`: damage per second inflicted when the scroll is used
3. Each second, the battle cycle goes:
   - Boss takes damage from all active spells
   - Boss regenerates `reg` health (but can't exceed `max`)
   - Player can use at most one scroll (only if conditions are met)
4. Boss is defeated when health ≤ 0 at the end of any second
5. We need to find if it's possible to defeat the boss, and if so, the minimal time and which scrolls to use when

## Key Insights

1. We need to use scrolls strategically - we want to use powerful scrolls when the boss has high health (so they're effective)
2. We should use scrolls in order of decreasing damage per second (`dmgi`) - more damage means we can defeat the boss faster
3. We can only use a scroll if the current health ≤ `powi`% of initial health
4. The boss takes damage from all active spells every second, then regenerates
5. We need to simulate the battle second by second

## Strategy

1. Sort scrolls by damage per second in descending order (greedy approach)
2. For each possible time, simulate the battle to see if we can defeat the boss
3. At each second, check if we can use any available scroll (health ≤ powi%)
4. Since we want minimal time, we can try different strategies or use dynamic programming, but given constraints (N, max, reg ≤ 1000), we can simulate up to a reasonable time limit

t=19: 81 - 10*(19-10) = 81 - 90 = -9 ≤ 0

Yes! At t=19, health ≤ 0.

The time is 19 seconds, and we used 2 scrolls.

So the sequence is: use scroll 1 at t=0, scroll 2 at t=10.

The key insight is that we need to find the optimal time to use each scroll.

Since we want minimal time, and we can only use one scroll per second, and scrolls have different power requirements, we should use high-damage scrolls when the boss health is high (so the power requirement is met).

But in this case, the scroll with dmg=11 has power requirement 100%, while the one with dmg=9 has requirement 90%. So we can only use the second scroll when health ≤ 90.

In our simulation above, at t=10, health was 91 (after damage and regeneration), which is 91% > 90%, so we couldn't use the second scroll at t=10.

But the example says we use it at t=10. Health before damage at t=10 was 91, which is 91% > 90%, so it shouldn't be allowed.

Wait, let me re-read: "the boss gets the damage from all the spells cast upon him, then he regenerates reg of health (at the same time he can’t have more than max of health), then the player may use another scroll"

So at the start of each second, the boss has some health, then:
1. Takes damage

3. Then player can use a scroll

So the health at the start of second t is what we use to check if we can use a scroll.

In the sequence:
- At t=0: start with health=100
- Use scroll 1 → so at t=0, we use it, then update health
- At end of t=0: health = 100 - 11 + 10 = 99

At start of t=1: health = 99

- Regeneration: 88 + 10 = 98

So health at start of second t is actually the health after the previous second's damage and regeneration.

The condition to use a scroll at time t is based on the health at the start of that second.

In the example, at start of t=10, health = 100 - 11*10 + 10*10 = 100 - 110 + 100 = 90

Let me calculate that:

At t=0: health after = 100 - 11 + 10 = 99
t=1: 99 - 11 + 10 = 98
t=2: 98 - 11 + 10 = 97
...
So after k seconds: health = 100 - k

At t=10: health = 100 - 10 = 90

Yes! So at start of t=10, health = 90, which is exactly 90% of 100, so we can use scroll 2 with powi=90.

After using scroll 2: total damage = 11 + 9 = 20

From t=10 onwards:
t=10: health = 90 - 20 + 10 = 80
t=11: 80 - 20 + 10 = 70
...
Each second: health decreases by 10

We need health ≤ 0 at end of some second.

After k seconds from t=10: health = 90 - 20*k + 10*k = 90 - 10*k

Set 90 - 10*k ≤ 0 → k ≥ 9

So at end of t=10 + 9 = t=19, health = 90 - 10*9 = 0

Perfect! So at t=19, health = 0, which is ≤ 0, so boss is defeated.

The solution is:

1. Sort scrolls by damage in descending order
2. Simulate over time, at each second, if health at start of second ≤ powi% of max, and we haven't used that scroll, use the highest damage scroll available
3. But in this case, we want to use the one with highest damage when possible
4. We need to find the minimal time when health ≤ 0

However, we might need to try different sequences. But given the constraints (max, reg ≤ 1000), and the fact that we're reducing health, the total time might not be too long.

Actually, since we can only use one scroll per second, and we have at most 1000 scrolls, we can simulate up to a reasonable time limit (say 10000 seconds).

But we need to be smart about it.

A greedy approach: 
- Use the highest damage scroll first (at earliest possible time)
- Then keep using other scrolls when possible

But is this optimal? Maybe not, but it should work for the examples.

Algorithm:
1. Read input
2. Store scrolls with (powi, dmgi, index)
3. Sort by dmgi descending (highest damage first)
4. Simulate time from 0 upwards
5. At each time t, calculate current health
6. Check if any scroll (not yet used) can be used (current health ≤ powi% of max)
7. If yes, use the one with highest dmgi among those available
8. Record when and which scroll
9. After using a scroll, update the damage rate
10. After each second, update health: health = max(0, health - total_damage) + reg, but capped at max
11. If at any point health ≤ 0, we found the solution
12. If we've used all scrolls and health > 0, then output "NO"

But we need to ensure that we don't use a scroll after the boss is defeated.

Also, the health at start of second t is what we use to check the condition.

In the simulation, at time t (before damage), the health is what we have.

Let me implement this.

```python
def solve():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read
    data = input().split()
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))  # (powi, dmgi, original_index)
    
    # Sort by damage in descending order
    scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
    
    # We'll simulate second by second
    # current_health: health at the start of the second (before damage)
    current_health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used_scrolls = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    used_set = set()
    
    # We need to find minimal time when health ≤ 0
    time = 0
    max_time = 10000  # reasonable upper bound
    
    while time < max_time:
        # Check if we can use any available scroll at this time
        available_scrolls = []
        for i, (powi, dmgi, orig_idx) in enumerate(scrolls):
            if i not in used_set and current_health <= (powi / 100.0) * max_health:
                available_scrolls.append((dmgi, orig_idx, i))
        
        # If no scroll available, check if boss is dead
        if not available_scrolls:
            # Check if current_health <= 0 (after damage and regen at start of this second)
            # But at start of second, before damage, we have current_health
            # After damage: current_health - total_damage
            # After regen: (current_health - total_damage) + reg, capped at max_health
            # But we only care about after the damage and regen
            health_after_damage_regen = max(0, current_health - total_damage) + reg
            if health_after_damage_regen <= 0:
                # Boss defeated at this time
                break
            else:
                # Move to next second
                # First apply damage and regeneration
                current_health = max(0, current_health - total_damage) + reg
                # But we can't exceed max_health
                current_health = min(current_health, max_health)
                time += 1
                continue
        
        # We have available scrolls, use the one with highest damage
        available_scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
        chosen_dmgi, chosen_orig_idx, chosen_idx = available_scrolls[0]
        
        # Use this scroll
        used_set.add(chosen_idx)
        used_scrolls.append((time, chosen_orig_idx))
        total_damage += chosen_dmgi
        
        # After using the scroll, the health at start of next second will be updated
        # First, apply damage to current health (at start of second)
        damage_taken = total_damage
        current_health_after_damage = current_health - damage_taken
        # Then regeneration
        current_health = min(max_health, current_health_after_damage + reg)
        
        # Move to next second
        time += 1
        
        # Check if boss is dead at this point
        if current_health <= 0:
            break
    
    if time >= max_time:
        print("NO")
    else:
        print("YES")
        print(time, len(used_scrolls))
        for t, scroll_idx in used_scrolls:
            print(t, scroll_idx)

solve()
```

```python
def solve():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read
    data = input().split()
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))  # (powi, dmgi, original_index)
    
    # Sort by damage in descending order
    scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
    
    # We'll simulate second by second
    current_health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used_scrolls = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    used_set = set()
    
    time = 0
    max_time = 10000  # reasonable upper bound
    
    while time < max_time:
        # At start of second time, check if any scroll can be used
        available_scrolls = []
        for i, (powi, dmgi, orig_idx) in enumerate(scrolls):
            if i not in used_set and current_health <= (powi / 100.0) * max_health:
                available_scrolls.append((dmgi, orig_idx, i))
        
        if not available_scrolls:
            # Check if at the end of this second, health ≤ 0
            # Damage happens: current_health - total_damage
            # Then regeneration: (current_health - total_damage) + reg
            health_after_damage_regen = current_health - total_damage
            health_after_regen = min(max_health, health_after_damage_regen + reg)
            if health_after_regen <= 0:
                break
            else:
                # Move to next second
                # The health at start of next second is health_after_regen
                current_health = health_after_regen
                time += 1
                continue
        
        # Use the best available scroll
        available_scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
        chosen_dmgi, chosen_orig_idx, chosen_idx = available_scrolls[0]

        used_scrolls.append((time, chosen_orig_idx))
        total_damage += chosen_dmgi

        health_after_damage = current_health - total_damage
        current_health = min(max_health, health_after_damage + reg)

        # Check if at end of this second, health ≤ 0
        if current_health <= 0:
            break

        print("NO")
    else:
        print("YES")
```

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        print("NO")
        return
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg = int(data[2])
    
    scrolls = []
    index = 3
    for i in range(n):
        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))  # (powi, dmgi, original_index)
    
    # Sort by damage in descending order to prioritize stronger scrolls
    scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
    
    current_health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used_scrolls = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    used_set = set()
    
    time = 0
    max_time = 10000  # reasonable upper bound
    
    while time < max_time:
        # Check if we can use any available scroll at this time
        available_scrolls = []
        for i, (powi, dmgi, orig_idx) in enumerate(scrolls):
            if i not in used_set and current_health <= (powi / 100.0) * max_health:
                available_scrolls.append((dmgi, orig_idx, i))
        
        if not available_scrolls:
            # Check if the boss is defeated at the end of this second
            damage_taken = total_damage
            health_after_damage = current_health - damage_taken
            health_after_regen = min(max_health, health_after_damage + reg)
            if health_after_regen <= 0:
                break
            else:
                # Move to next second
                current_health = health_after_regen
                time += 1
                continue
        
        # Use the highest damage scroll available
        available_scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)
        chosen_dmgi, chosen_orig_idx, chosen_idx = available_scrolls[0]
        
        used_set.add(chosen_idx)
        used_scrolls.append((time, chosen_orig_idx))
        total_damage += chosen_dmgi
        
        # Apply damage and regeneration at the end of this second
        damage_taken = total_damage
        health_after_damage = current_health - damage_taken
        current_health = min(max_health, health_after_damage + reg)
        
        time += 1
        
        # Check if boss is defeated at end of this second
        if current_health <= 0:
            break
    
    if time >= max_time:
        print("NO")
    else:
        print("YES")
        print(time, len(used_scrolls))
        for t, scroll_idx in used_scrolls:
            print(t, scroll_idx)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        print("NO")
        return
    
    n = int(data[0])
    max_health = int(data[1])
    reg = int(data[2])

        powi = int(data[index])
        dmgi = int(data[index + 1])
        index += 2
        scrolls.append((powi, dmgi, i + 1))  # (powi, dmgi, original_index)
    
    # Sort by damage in descending order to prioritize stronger scrolls
    scrolls.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)
    
    current_health = max_health
    total_damage = 0
    used_scrolls = []  # list of (time, scroll_index)
    used_set = set()
    
    time = 0
    max_time = 10000  # reasonable upper bound
    
    while time < max_time:
        # Check if we can use any available scroll at this time
        available_scrolls = []
        for i, (powi, dmgi, orig_idx) in enumerate(scrolls):
            if i not in used_set and current_health <= (powi / 100.0) * max_health:
                available_scrolls.append((dmgi, orig_idx, i))
        
        if not available_scrolls:

                continue
